4 Frame structure and physical resources

4.1 General

Throughout this specification, unless otherwise noted, the size of various fields in the time domain is expressed in time units [FIG:59aa1a5ee20d175039c6] where [FIG:7a527e83b81a9e940b96] Hz and [FIG:d96ed85f93a9efa4f1a2] . The constant [FIG:28833e6cc7bfa9bc0170] where [FIG:b8e586d8d372d6b868c2] , [FIG:288a024af88793cb6c92] and [FIG:0bf343f701b59894052f] .

4.2 Numerologies

Multiple OFDM numerologies are supported as given by Table 4.2-1 where [FIG:f57a676a1ac4a9cfaec3] and the cyclic prefix for a bandwidth part are obtained from the higher-layer parameter subcarrierSpacing and cyclicPrefix, respectively.

Table 4.2-1: Supported transmission numerologies.

[FIG:fd6cea4a19be10d8b645] | [FIG:ef8c0e7b31e3d793b4ee] | Cyclic prefix
0 | 15 | Normal
1 | 30 | Normal
2 | 60 | Normal, Extended
3 | 120 | Normal
4 | 240 | Normal

4.3 Frame structure

4.3.1 Frames and subframes

Downlink and uplink transmissions are organized into frames with [FIG:acb66c2d20373c6d4c21] duration, each consisting of ten subframes of [FIG:7fda89ace7617b7fdbe3] duration. The number of consecutive OFDM symbols per subframe is [FIG:ec4263a60fa1d87c9046] . Each frame is divided into two equally-sized half-frames of five subframes each with half-frame 0 consisting of subframes 0 – 4 and half-frame 1 consisting of subframes 5 – 9.

There is one set of frames in the uplink and one set of frames in the downlink on a carrier.

Uplink frame number [FIG:5de8b3a05ff012134931] for transmission from the UE shall start [FIG:665f6e50cc779880af1c] before the start of the corresponding downlink frame at the UE where [FIG:398089bfb1bf5a475f26] depends on the frequency band according to [38.133].

[FIG:cfa71e00b3c6b99f852a]

Figure 4.3.1-1: Uplink-downlink timing relation.

4.3.2 Slots

For subcarrier spacing configuration [FIG:2340340fd2656577252e] , slots are numbered [FIG:7ead71ef8b9d8d8b27c5] in increasing order within a subframe and [FIG:e05688c9d625782634bb] in increasing order within a frame. There are [FIG:04b874d3cad4d18c2afc] consecutive OFDM symbols in a slot where [FIG:46859583ea932a4cca49] depends on the cyclic prefix as given by Tables 4.3.2-1 and 4.3.2-2. The start of slot [FIG:55f25908e95a07aeef5c] in a subframe is aligned in time with the start of OFDM symbol [FIG:feb8aace94cc158e5623] in the same subframe.

OFDM symbols in a slot can be classified as 'downlink', 'flexible', or 'uplink'. Signaling of slot formats is described in subclause 11.1 of [5, TS 38.213].

In a slot in a downlink frame, the UE shall assume that downlink transmissions only occur in 'downlink' or 'flexible' symbols.

In a slot in an uplink frame, the UE shall only transmit in 'uplink' or 'flexible' symbols.

A UE not capable of full-duplex communication is not expected to transmit in the uplink earlier than [EQ:6ebecaf1f75408480a69] after the end of the last received downlink symbol in the same cell where [EQ:bf897d69a21ff6499646] is given by [TS 38.101].

Table 4.3.2-1: Number of OFDM symbols per slot, slots per frame, and slots per subframe for normal cyclic prefix.

[FIG:6dbc7a46990e70eb3a64] | [FIG:f330673ef6039405da08] | [FIG:af38cfc86b77246510de] | [FIG:74ab2bebb71aaa0eaf37]
0 | 14 | 10 | 1
1 | 14 | 20 | 2
2 | 14 | 40 | 4
3 | 14 | 80 | 8
4 | 14 | 160 | 16

Table 4.3.2-2: Number of OFDM symbols per slot, slots per frame, and slots per subframe for extended cyclic prefix.

[FIG:ec701c4cf83c298b508d] | [FIG:0eba35faf75c0f53373f] | [FIG:c6324d7c255d0e3400c7] | [FIG:caf8f181222da51b74ba]
2 | 12 | 40 | 4

4.4 Physical resources

4.4.1 Antenna ports

An antenna port is defined such that the channel over which a symbol on the antenna port is conveyed can be inferred from the channel over which another symbol on the same antenna port is conveyed.

For DM-RS associated with a PDSCH, the channel over which a PDSCH symbol on one antenna port is conveyed can be inferred from the channel over which a DM-RS symbol on the same antenna port is conveyed only if the two symbols are within the same resource as the scheduled PDSCH, in the same slot, and in the same PRG as described in clause 5.1.2.3 of [6, TS 38.214].

Two antenna ports are said to be quasi co-located if the large-scale properties of the channel over which a symbol on one antenna port is conveyed can be inferred from the channel over which a symbol on the other antenna port is conveyed. The large-scale properties include one or more of delay spread, Doppler spread, Doppler shift, average gain, average delay, and spatial Rx parameters.

4.4.2 Resource grid

For each numerology and carrier, a resource grid of [FIG:1f01084139281a443b5b] subcarriers and [FIG:9f8bdefde259fe5f6c51] OFDM symbols is defined, starting at common resource block [FIG:9451c0cdb597aca15a7a] indicated by higher-layer signalling. There is one set of resource grids per transmission direction (uplink or downlink) with the subscript [FIG:086ad752a41bf2bbba3d] set to DL and UL for downlink and uplink, respectively. When there is no risk for confusion, the subscript [FIG:c8d3804a7dd34dc9bc5a] may be dropped. There is one resource grid for a given antenna port [FIG:4da922e301f02b7415f0] , subcarrier spacing configuration [FIG:99cbd2de686266fe04ca] , and transmission direction (downlink or uplink).

The carrier bandwidth [EQ:cfda2d03dac21a2ac294] for subcarrier spacing configuration [EQ:f8954c1c80918d14c68b] is given by the higher-layer parameter carrierBandwidth in the SCS-SpecificCarrier IE. The starting position [EQ:ff65188d52a58417a7b9] for subcarrier spacing configuration [EQ:9c1bcad42c3a1bed98aa] is given by the higher-layer parameter offsetToCarrier in the SCS-SpecificCarrier IE.

The frequency location of a subcarrier refers to the center frequency of that subcarrier.

For the downlink, the higher-layer parameter DCsubcarrierDL indicates the location of the transmitter DC subcarrier in the downlink for each of the numerologies configured in the downlink. Values in the range 0 – 3299 represent the number of the DC subcarrier and the value 3300 indicates that the DC subcarrier is located outside the resource grid.

For the uplink, the higher-layer parameter DCsubcarrierUL indicate the location of the transmitter DC subcarrier in the uplink for each of the configured bandwidth parts, including whether the DC subcarrier location is offset by 7.5 kHz relative to the center of the indicated subcarrier or not. Values in the range 0 – 3299 represent the number of the DC subcarrier, the value 3300 indicates that the DC subcarrier is located outside the resource grid, and the value 3301 indicates that the position of the DC subcarrier in the uplink is undetermined.

4.4.3 Resource elements

Each element in the resource grid for antenna port [FIG:95005bc4fbe45ef4d769] and subcarrier spacing configuration [FIG:b0f0553f7075df909755] is called a resource element and is uniquely identified by [FIG:65fa67dc3743d35ac573] where [FIG:79fc6d44e7b64981c1c4] is the index in the frequency domain and [FIG:6d24ea081c5295b4058e] refers to the symbol position in the time domain relative to some reference point. Resource element [FIG:1958bdb0d1931ce16e12] corresponds to a physical resource and the complex value [FIG:b6c875d18acd5f94d494] . When there is no risk for confusion, or no particular antenna port or subcarrier spacing is specified, the indices [FIG:21df732b6d7777c4a88f] and [FIG:54b5727a20bbfcb8daf9] may be dropped, resulting in [FIG:79f24ae93d731d4d5f2c] or [FIG:e3f8dff436ac967c9cc4] .

4.4.4 Resource blocks

4.4.4.1 General

A resource block is defined as [FIG:c17003557506adbe5df6] consecutive subcarriers in the frequency domain.

4.4.4.2 Point A

Point A serves as a common reference point for resource block grids and is obtained from:

- offsetToPointA for a PCell downlink represents the frequency offset between point A and the lowest subcarrier of the lowest resource block of the SS/PBCH block used by the UE for initial cell selection, expressed in units of resource blocks assuming 15 kHz subcarrier spacing for FR1 and 60 kHz subcarrier spacing for FR2;

- absoluteFrequencyPointA for all other cases where absoluteFrequencyPointA represents the frequency-location of point A expressed as in ARFCN.

4.4.4.3 Common resource blocks

Common resource blocks are numbered from 0 and upwards in the frequency domain for subcarrier spacing configuration [FIG:cdbb2284dacf0b357594] . The center of subcarrier 0 of common resource block 0 for subcarrier spacing configuration [FIG:7640b3bff39078ce1af8] coincides with 'point A'.

The relation between the common resource block number [FIG:917a611f296cfacbf009] in the frequency domain and resource elements [FIG:3f8cb9b16b18b2f77576] for subcarrier spacing configuration [FIG:dbaee8bed92161225455] is given by

[FIG:842f40ec93835eabdf22]

where [FIG:fb11e31b67b880b4d23c] is defined relative to point A such that [FIG:3f74038cd5fc112aeb7c] corresponds to the subcarrier centered around point A.

4.4.4.4 Physical resource blocks

Physical resource blocks are defined within a bandwidth part and numbered from 0 to [FIG:06deb04495b4a64515a4] where [FIG:b10af8ae93bef98417f3] is the number of the bandwidth part. The relation between the physical resource block [FIG:095681a1cc38d9b9e543] in bandwidth part [FIG:d682f30e8664212ff243] and the common resource block [FIG:d6547c74e0b98f3013fe] is given by

[FIG:95b72420e0e2df148889]

where [FIG:839b191f65ee69f3960e] is the common resource block where bandwidth part starts relative to common resource block 0.

4.4.4.5 Virtual resource blocks

Virtual resource blocks are defined within a bandwidth part and numbered from 0 to [FIG:767048650202417db40b] where [FIG:ec891af38774400480b9] is the number of the bandwidth part.

4.4.5 Bandwidth part

A bandwidth part is a subset of contiguous common resource blocks defined in subclause 4.4.4.3 for a given numerology [FIG:786526755df4a25a6df4] in bandwidth part [FIG:4bdb5c1572d265b615be] on a given carrier. The starting position [FIG:55afb40dfc6a9011702b] and the number of resource blocks [FIG:c9b2bbeda150a105240e] in a bandwidth part shall fulfil [FIG:e92388dee2ffb9a2508b] and [FIG:fcb76ce2c5df0bf74746] , respectively. Configuration of a bandwidth part is described in clause 12 of [5, TS 38.213].

A UE can be configured with up to four bandwidth parts in the downlink with a single downlink bandwidth part being active at a given time. The UE is not expected to receive PDSCH, PDCCH, or CSI-RS (except for RRM) outside an active bandwidth part.

A UE can be configured with up to four bandwidth parts in the uplink with a single uplink bandwidth part being active at a given time. If a UE is configured with a supplementary uplink, the UE can in addition be configured with up to four bandwidth parts in the supplementary uplink with a single supplementary uplink bandwidth part being active at a given time. The UE shall not transmit PUSCH or PUCCH outside an active bandwidth part. For an active cell, the UE shall not transmit SRS outside an active bandwidth part.

Unless otherwise noted, the description in this specification applies to each of the bandwidth parts.

4.5 Carrier aggregation

Transmissions in multiple cells can be aggregated. Unless otherwise noted, the description in this specification applies to each of the serving cells.

5.3 OFDM baseband signal generation

5.3.1 OFDM baseband signal generation for all channels except PRACH

The time-continuous signal [FIG:ca3b2ffb08642c6095e9] on antenna port [FIG:40e9dce9fc0981369219] and subcarrier spacing configuration [FIG:a7f9caf86dde79c6511f] for OFDM symbol [FIG:a44cad1122a6c77d9e8e] in a subframe for any physical channel or signal except PRACH is defined by

[FIG:1b34d054fc920bff2d81]

where [FIG:5010a49b465e2bba918c] is the time within the subframe,

6 Uplink

6.1 Overview

6.1.1 Overview of physical channels

An uplink physical channel corresponds to a set of resource elements carrying information originating from higher layers. The following uplink physical channels are defined:

- Physical Uplink Shared Channel, PUSCH

- Physical Uplink Control Channel, PUCCH

- Physical Random Access Channel, PRACH

6.1.2 Overview of physical signals

An uplink physical signal is used by the physical layer but does not carry information originating from higher layers. The following uplink physical signals are defined:

- Demodulation reference signals, DM-RS

- Phase-tracking reference signals, PT-RS

- Sounding reference signal, SRS

6.2 Physical resources

The frame structure and physical resources the UE shall use when transmitting in the uplink transmissions are defined in Clause 4.

The following antenna ports are defined for the uplink:

- Antenna ports starting with 0 for PUSCH and associated demodulation reference signals

- Antenna ports starting with 1000 for SRS

- Antenna ports starting with 2000 for PUCCH

- Antenna port 4000 for PRACH

6.3 Physical channels

6.3.1 Physical uplink shared channel

6.3.1.1 Scrambling

For the single codeword [FIG:f78fc5ef48bfdc8cd666] , the block of bits [FIG:a994073badf67ac05cc6] , where [FIG:ce4db377b14ec1762eb0] is the number of bits in codeword [FIG:e0927bb4b8c457b4ab23] transmitted on the physical channel, shall be scrambled prior to modulation, resulting in a block of scrambled bits [FIG:0eba9554ccd4e7b17631] according to the following pseudo code

6.3.1.6 Mapping to virtual resource blocks

For each of the antenna ports used for transmission of the PUSCH, the block of complex-valued symbols [FIG:57bb30497319fdb5b8c8] shall be multiplied with the amplitude scaling factor [FIG:a10f484d111c3d5ab921] in order to conform to the transmit power specified in [5, TS 38.213] and mapped in sequence starting with [FIG:38b38e6c3f3e388cfe54] to resource elements [FIG:cd927589115fa2eaa534] in the virtual resource blocks assigned for transmission which meet all of the following criteria:

- they are in the virtual resource blocks assigned for transmission, and

- the corresponding resource elements in the corresponding physical resource blocks are not used for transmission of the associated DM-RS, PT-RS, or DM-RS intended for other co-scheduled UEs as described in clause 6.4.1.1.3

The mapping to resource elements [FIG:a52c41f6d7e607639c9d] allocated for PUSCH according to [6, TS 38.214] shall be in increasing order of first the index [FIG:c87261a943a1efee8ca5] over the assigned virtual resource blocks, where [FIG:4b85d38a2dec88790a4c] is the first subcarrier in the lowest-numbered virtual resource block assigned for transmission, and then the index [FIG:cbfd644185c782254228] , with the starting position given by [6, TS 38.214].

6.3.1.7 Mapping from virtual to physical resource blocks

Virtual resource blocks shall be mapped to physical resource blocks according to non-interleaved mapping.

For non-interleaved VRB-to-PRB mapping, virtual resource block [FIG:bdbf12e40af972705260] is mapped to physical resource block [FIG:df69cf2392cc795b7c65] .

6.3.2 Physical uplink control channel

6.3.2.1 General

The physical uplink control channel supports multiple formats as shown in Table 6.3.2.1-1. In case intra-slot frequency hopping is configured for PUCCH formats 1, 3, or 4 according to clause 9.2.1 of [5, TS38.213], the number of symbols in the first hop is given by [FIG:8ee0ae76c066e9632426] where [FIG:e77a5a9064f1b4ad07cf] is the length of the PUCCH transmission in OFDM symbols.

Table 6.3.2.1-1: PUCCH formats.

PUCCH format | Length in OFDM symbols [FIG:5c389b514081db6dd323] | Number of bits
0 | 1 – 2 | ≤2
1 | 4 – 14 | ≤2
2 | 1 – 2 | >2
3 | 4 – 14 | >2
4 | 4 – 14 | >2

6.3.2.2 Sequence and cyclic shift hopping

PUCCH formats 0, 1, 3, and 4 use sequences [FIG:2f15fd2a87ed93a569d6] given by clause 5.2.2 with [FIG:79d4884e78324fedb69c] where the sequence group [FIG:41e27bd052177804045c] and the sequence number [FIG:1f7df04add71a28f4b8b] depend on the sequence hopping in clause 6.3.2.2.1 and the cyclic shift [FIG:f26270c02427f4fe6bd1] depends on the cyclic shift hopping in clause 6.3.2.2.2.

6.3.2.2.1 Group and sequence hopping

The sequence group [FIG:baf2007405b66273e30a] and the sequence number [FIG:8f0e744f0f873c3ed415] within the group depends on the higher-layer parameter pucch-GroupHopping:

- if pucch-GroupHopping equals 'neither'

[FIG:ae745d402e351e07096f]

where [FIG:96c23521ced419206661] is given by the higher-layer parameter hoppingId.

- if pucch-GroupHopping equals 'enable'

[FIG:fc9960e52db0b90823a1]

where the pseudo-random sequence [FIG:75f4d4fb53b1d3324d42] is defined by clause 5.2.1 and shall be initialized at the beginning of each radio frame with [FIG:437583b5c88e415c94ea] where [FIG:093a7f72a9609bd7af22] is given by the higher-layer parameter hoppingId.

- if pucch-GroupHopping equals 'disable'

[FIG:4582ae77e749631d58a5]

where the pseudo-random sequence [FIG:17265fef7d9934d9c477] is defined by clause 5.2.1 and shall be initialized at the beginning of each radio frame with [EQ:8e8287231673ad9cd419] where [FIG:eba5f2dcc504b3f0a41d] is given by the higher-layer parameter hoppingId.

The frequency hopping index [FIG:5f3859faad0d92682b4a] if intra-slot frequency hopping is disabled by the higher-layer parameter PUCCH-frequency-hopping. There are 64 preambles defined in each time-frequency PRACH occasion, enumerated in increasing order of first increasing cyclic shift [FIG:2e14dd4f0686d71b191e] of a logical root sequence, and then in increasing order of the logical root sequence index, starting with the index obtained from the higher-layer parameter prach-RootSequenceIndex. Additional preamble sequences, in case 64 preambles cannot be generated from a single root Zadoff-Chu sequence, are obtained from the root sequences with the consecutive logical indexes until all the 64 sequences are found. The logical root sequence order is cyclic; the logical index 0 is consecutive to 837 when [FIG:5d15f5471e72ce677d68] and is consecutive to 137 when [FIG:0e4eeffc32aa2c36938a] . 6.3.3.2 Mapping to physical resources

The preamble sequence shall be mapped to physical resources according to

[FIG:2df0b0f4715a8d126dcc]

where [FIG:fa57a23dcd76dac4b29d] is an amplitude scaling factor in order to conform to the transmit power specified in [5, TS38.213], and [FIG:dd7ce90a7ccf1202e2c0] is the antenna port. Baseband signal generation shall be done according to clause 5.3 using the parameters in Table 6.3.3.1-1 or Table 6.3.3.1-2 with [FIG:99ae0b5698d9391c20b4] given by Table 6.3.3.2-1.

Random access preambles can only be transmitted in the time resources given by the higher-layer parameter prach-ConfigurationIndex according to Tables 6.3.3.2-2 to 6.3.3.2-4 and depends on FR1 or FR2 and the spectrum type as defined in [8, TS38.104].

Random access preambles can only be transmitted in the frequency resources given by the higher-layer parameter msg1-FrequencyStart. The PRACH frequency resources [FIG:e2c4ba9200a431bf7f5f] , where M equals the higher-layer parameter msg1-FDM, are numbered in increasing order within the initial active uplink bandwidth part during initial access, starting from the lowest frequency. Otherwise, [FIG:37500e7cdeda70ee5abf] are numbered in increasing order within the active uplink bandwidth part, starting from the lowest frequency.

For the purpose of slot numbering in the tables, the following subcarrier spacing shall be assumed:

- 15 kHz for FR1

- 60 kHz for FR2.

For handover purposes within the same frequency range in paired spectrum with [EQ:5de485e5415b4465ac28] , the UE may assume the absolute value of the time difference between radio frame [EQ:fb2ca21a8410bfb02ff6] in the current cell and radio frame [EQ:47835b5d6e3d4827c9f0] in the target cell is less than [EQ:c824f299c6d423aff2a7] if any of the following conditions are fulfilled:

- the entries in Tables 6.3.3.2-2 and 6.3.3.2-3 where [EQ:05e50e65030cbbfebe78]

- the entries in Tables 6.3.3.2-2 and 6.3.3.2-3 where [EQ:a4f4b54768a46ada2f28] and and the association period in Table 8.1-1 of [38.213] is not equal to 1

6.4.1.1.1.1 Sequence generation when transform precoding is disabled

If transform precoding for PUSCH is not enabled, the sequence [FIG:55a3c8b6fb75dab9b846] shall be generated according to

[FIG:548464625ecba5f0b2ad] .

where the pseudo-random sequence [FIG:81246e8e3fb177ad5b3d] is defined in clause 5.2.1. The pseudo-random sequence generator shall be initialized with

[FIG:fb26edc4fa9ecf63a9d0]

where [FIG:a862c11cc53b1ca8f4f8] is the OFDM symbol number within the slot, [FIG:5f33a1133bb739187bf7] is the slot number within a frame, and

- [FIG:1683c75b850a13b97992] are given by the higher-layer parameters scramblingID0 and scramblingID1, respectively, in the DMRS-UplinkConfig IE if provided and the PUSCH is scheduled by DCI format 0_1;

- [FIG:1f2a9a9362ebb2aed558] is given by the higher-layer parameter scramblingID0 in the DMRS-UplinkConfig IE if provided and the PUSCH is scheduled by DCI format 0_0 with the CRC scrambled by C-RNTI;

- [FIG:a51b5a4b1328cff6bdec] otherwise.

The quantity [EQ:4eb2d2e8853e0fa4e4cc] is indicated by the DM-RS initialization field, if present, in the DCI associated with the PUSCH transmission if DCI format 0_1 in [4, TS 38.212] is used, otherwise [EQ:a82f4826bda7e2910cc7] .

[FIG:0aff122c7bd3ed1ad810]

The sequence group [FIG:04ec226fb627509256df] , where [FIG:a2eab4b9b5815bea500e] is given by

- [FIG:337481c86ac6db9e4212] if [FIG:d15dec6d515089eae687] is configured by the higher-layer parameter nPUSCH-Identity in the DMRS-UplinkConfig IE and the PUSCH is not a msg3 PUSCH according to clause 8.3 in [5, TS 38.213].

- [EQ:c73cc81267b1bb0af137] otherwise

- if neither group, nor sequence hopping shall be used

[FIG:fbf9c94a472fc2ada588]

- if group hopping but not sequence hopping shall be used

[EQ:1b5b3f33934e0e2c50e3]

The UE shall transmit phase-tracking reference signals only in the resource blocks and OFDM symbols used for the PUSCH, and only if the procedure in [6, TS 38.214] indicates that phase-tracking reference signals are being used.

An SRS resource is configured by the SRS-Resource IE and consists of

- [FIG:217e55ae2b0b10eb3556] antenna ports [FIG:13465dd1c1c41488d43d] , [FIG:8a52611ed4946570fab5] , given by the higher layer parameter nrofSRS-Ports

- [FIG:507980ee47a18f6bb165] consecutive OFDM symbols given by the field nrofSymbols contained in the higher layer parameter resourceMapping

- [FIG:81090c19c78c90afa2eb] , the starting position in the time domain given by [FIG:0f4943346cebe9b9cc36] where the offset [FIG:7cefb969a1faaa1d393d] counts symbols backwards from the end of the slot and is given by the field startPosition contained in the higher layer parameter resourceMapping and [FIG:13e3750308b8b331b9ee]

6.4.1.4.4 Sounding reference signal slot configuration

For an SRS resource configured as periodic or semi-persistent by the higher-layer parameter resourceType, a periodicity [FIG:4b56a4023b134f091a81] (in slots) and slot offset [FIG:91ef648ad265881277de] are configured according to the higher-layer parameter periodicityAndOffset-p or periodicityAndOffset-ap. Candidate slots in which the configured SRS resource may be used for SRS transmission are the slots satisfying

[FIG:c84487fe9d262889a0df]

SRS may be transmitted only if all OFDM symbols corresponding to the configured SRS resource are semi-statically configured as 'uplink' or 'flexible' in the candidate slot and SRS transmission is allowed by the procedure in clause 11.1 of [5, TS 38.213].

7 Downlink

7.1 Overview

7.1.1 Overview of physical channels

A downlink physical channel corresponds to a set of resource elements carrying information originating from higher layers. The following downlink physical channels are defined:

- Physical Downlink Shared Channel, PDSCH

- Physical Broadcast Channel, PBCH

- Physical Downlink Control Channel, PDCCH.

7.1.2 Overview of physical signals

A downlink physical signal corresponds to a set of resource elements used by the physical layer but does not carry information originating from higher layers.

The following downlink physical signals are defined:

- Demodulation reference signals, DM-RS

- Phase-tracking reference signals, PT-RS

- Channel-state information reference signal, CSI-RS

- Primary synchronization signal, PSS

- Secondary synchronization signal, SSS

7.2 Physical resources

The frame structure and physical resources the UE shall assume when receiving downlink transmissions are defined in Clause 4.

The following antenna ports are defined for the downlink:

- Antenna ports starting with 1000 for PDSCH

- Antenna ports starting with 2000 for PDCCH

- Antenna ports starting with 3000 for channel-state information reference signals

- Antenna ports starting with 4000 for SS/PBCH block transmission

The UE shall not assume that two antenna ports are quasi co-located with respect to any QCL type unless specified otherwise.

7.3 Physical channels

7.3.1 Physical downlink shared channel

7.3.1.1 Scrambling

Up to two codewords [FIG:8e9422b5e4dd67b1aeb0] can be transmitted. In case of single-codeword transmission, [FIG:133b65498aa62e03a73e] .

For each codeword [FIG:5fced6336e2853b78b38] , the UE shall assume the block of bits [FIG:f4dcc3a925c408db7e33] , where [FIG:d4b5dbcf3e51888ca2eb] is the number of bits in codeword [FIG:a75b49baa0800bce2010] transmitted on the physical channel, are scrambled prior to modulation, resulting in a block of scrambled bits [FIG:d91a0bdadf00bd9423eb] according to

[FIG:e9b2437e9e34c5960ada]

where the scrambling sequence [FIG:3a5ecfc574e705e5fe78] is given by clause 5.2.1. The scrambling sequence generator shall be initialized with

[FIG:3d5d098e19de87a516da]

where

- [FIG:1fddcc12180f32563af5] equals the higher-layer parameter dataScramblingIdentityPDSCH if configured and the RNTI equals the C-RNTI or CS-RNTI, and the transmission is not scheduled using DCI format 1_0 in a common search space,

- [FIG:21ba6986ef0d38959d6e] otherwise

and where [FIG:c37e64318cbe194133ad] corresponds to the RNTI associated with the PDSCH transmission as described in clause 5.1 of [6, TS 38.214].

7.3.1.2 Modulation

For each codeword [FIG:224a06873dba8a70c21a] , the UE shall assume the block of scrambled bits [FIG:e768744ed3edd3bd1be3] are modulated as described in clause 5.1 using one of the modulation schemes in Table 7.3.1.2-1, resulting in a block of complex-valued modulation symbols [FIG:341cfb49f849e8b2964d] .

256QAM | 8

7.3.1.5 Mapping to virtual resource blocks

The UE shall, for each of the antenna ports used for transmission of the physical channel, assume the block of complex-valued symbols [FIG:845d42f46017ba9127b3] conform to the downlink power allocation specified in [6, TS 38.214] and are mapped in sequence starting with [FIG:c59a986f5d3bf177fc90] to resource elements [FIG:1cc39ec703c92653b0e8] in the virtual resource blocks assigned for transmission which meet all of the following criteria:

- they are in the virtual resource blocks assigned for transmission.

- they are declared as available for PDSCH according to clause 5.1.4 of [6, TS 38.214]

- the corresponding resource elements in the corresponding physical resource blocks are

- not used for transmission of the associated DM-RS or DM-RS intended for other co-scheduled UEs as described in clause 7.4.1.1.2

- not used for non-zero-power CSI-RS according to clause 7.4.1.5, except for non-zero-power CSI-RSs configured by the higher-layer parameter CSI-RS-Resource-Mobility in the MeasObjectNR IE.

- not used for PT-RS according to clause 7.4.1.2

- not declared as 'not available for PDSCH according to clause 5.1.4 of [6, TS 38.214]

Any common resource block partially or fully overlapping with an SS/PBCH block shall be viewed as occupied and assumed not used for transmission of PDSCH in the OFDM symbols where SS/PBCH block is transmitted.

The mapping to resource elements [FIG:623deda1cb526ba78126] allocated for PDSCH according to [6, TS 38.214] and not reserved for other purposes shall be in increasing order of first the index [FIG:74d6e734e289f5b0377d] over the assigned virtual resource blocks, where [FIG:e9ec271b965f419299ac] is the first subcarrier in the lowest-numbered virtual resource block assigned for transmission, and then the index [FIG:d97a395b97f85de67a1e] .

7.3.1.6 Mapping from virtual to physical resource blocks

The UE shall assume the virtual resource blocks are mapped to physical resource blocks according to the indicated mapping scheme, non-interleaved or interleaved mapping. If no mapping scheme is indicated, the UE shall assume non-interleaved mapping.

For non-interleaved VRB-to-PRB mapping, virtual resource block [FIG:e1e244cc2d3599c703a4] is mapped to physical resource block [FIG:e055d835273607c195eb] , except for PDSCH transmissions scheduled with DCI format 1_0 in a common search space in which case virtual resource block [EQ:26932da278d207176ac5] is mapped to physical resource block [EQ:6bbc1025c60ad584d048] where [EQ:4f7e324dc25e7935a6a2] is the lowest-numbered physical resource block in the control resource set where the corresponding DCI was received.

For interleaved VRB-to-PRB mapping, the mapping process is defined in terms of resource block bundles:

- The set of [FIG:cf890e5e4fa25a056f01] resource blocks in bandwidth part [FIG:5681af89f5827d9f4cce] with starting position [FIG:f2b98b849992dfd84c01] are divided into [FIG:fee224cf36e6b783cf35] resource-block bundles in increasing order of the resource-block number and bundle number where [FIG:9019da66fc5f7945bfee] is the bundle size for bandwidth part [FIG:d68c3a8b8d22d06babe2] provided by the higher-layer parameter vrb-ToPRB-Interleaver and

- resource block bundle 0 consists of [FIG:fef1e97a2c6ab01cc11c] resource blocks,

- resource block bundle [FIG:0ebc018499590952cb81] consists of [FIG:2e760937c18c37d914f2] resource blocks if [FIG:e35af74525d2d3aae00c] and [FIG:b47b5fa8e409d0f59141] resource blocks otherwise,

- all other resource block bundles consists of [FIG:00f87fc1bd913e637d01] resource blocks.

- Virtual resource blocks in the interval [FIG:ce30dcd64391c0f10934] are mapped to physical resource blocks according to

- virtual resource block bundle [FIG:151b50d63a08ddfe68fe] is mapped to physical resource block bundle [FIG:9b19b02f3a900aa5d782]

- virtual resource block bundle [FIG:98d8466784b10e680ba2] is mapped to physical resource block bundle [FIG:ba46852f34c8baf5749b] where

[FIG:90398c9b2d5d8f668d39]

- The UE is not expected to be configured with [FIG:5da71633c03fd0c572e3] simultaneosuly with a PRG size of 4 as defined in [6, TS 38.214]

- If no bundle size is configured, the UE shall assume [FIG:b1b2a37549f0d766c652] .

The UE may assume that the same precoding in the frequency domain is used within a PRB bundle and the bundle size is determined by clause 5.1.2.3 in [6, TS 38.214]. The UE shall not make any assumption that the same precoding is used for different bundles of common resource blocks.

7.3.2 Physical downlink control channel (PDCCH)

7.3.2.1 Control-channel element (CCE)

A physical downlink control channel consists of one or more control-channel elements (CCEs) as indicated in Table 7.3.2.1-1.

Table 7.3.2.1-1: Supported PDCCH aggregation levels.

Aggregation level | Number of CCEs
1 | 1
2 | 2
4 | 4
8 | 8
16 | 16

7.3.2.2 Control-resource set (CORESET)

A control-resource set consists of [FIG:fb6134a44bb58c820281] resource blocks in the frequency domain, given by the higher-layer parameter frequencyDomainResources in the ControlResourceSet IE, and [FIG:30dbc1367cbde613ce7c] symbols in the time domain, given by the higher-layer parameter duration in the ControlResourceSet IE, where [FIG:30f0dcd17ee46f5d0da5] is supported only if higher-layer parameter dmrs-TypeA-Position equals 3.

A control-channel element consists of 6 resource-element groups (REGs) where a resource-element group equals one resource block during one OFDM symbol. Resource-element groups within a control-resource set are numbered in increasing order in a time-first manner, starting with 0 for the first OFDM symbol and the lowest-numbered resource block in the control resource set.

A UE can be configured with multiple control-resource sets. Each control-resource set is associated with one CCE-to-REG mapping only.

The CCE-to-REG mapping for a control-resource set can be interleaved or non-interleaved, configured by the higher-layer parameter cce-REG-MappingType in the ControlResourceSet IE, and is described by REG bundles:

- REG bundle [FIG:8d9f80f53d905264f2f7] is defined as REGs [FIG:f2d977d012a49c18326a] where [FIG:b1aa3d6ab835cc40160c] is the REG bundle size, [FIG:5b8613da0cdaacc77c9b] , and [FIG:aabd7a6823d803d6dfc2] is the number of REGs in the CORESET

- CCE [FIG:f08d1636a041a1c70439] consists of REG bundles [FIG:f0102349208d9792ca67] where [FIG:0c73b78e1be3a247bab5] is an interleaver

For non-interleaved CCE-to-REG mapping, [FIG:acd1134cb78e6cf440e2] and [FIG:d2c3f23c0b87a4ac6cd2] .

For interleaved CCE-to-REG mapping, [FIG:90a4d81bc26bb0ba1145] for [FIG:07cd3d20b2c370491c51] and [FIG:9f9bb118c84c9986909a] for [FIG:8ee39c9450b9179fd103] where [FIG:c0fa40f11374f29b3bf8] is configured by the higher-layer parameter reg-BundleSize. The interleaver is defined by

[FIG:b60b5ff4d44573bb8ddd]

where [FIG:dc22661e9586d493731f] is given by the higher-layer parameter interleaverSize and where

- [FIG:4e4e410588c4f92e6a0b] for a PDCCH transmitted in a CORESET configured by the PBCH or SIB1, and

- otherwise [FIG:02d731f26633bb7eae21] is given by the higher-layer parameter shiftIndex.

The UE is not expected to handle configurations resulting in the quantity [FIG:a7ac4183567464cf2060] not being an integer.

For both interleaved and non-interleaved mapping, the UE may assume

- the same precoding being used within a REG bundle if the higher-layer parameter precoderGranularity equals sameAsREG-bundle

- the same precoding being used across the all resource-element groups within the set of contiguous resource blocks in the CORESET if the higher-layer parameter precoderGranularity equals allContiguousRBs

For a CORESET configured by PBCH the UE may assume interleaved mapping, [FIG:8ff2b3c935cb5b2c6c0e] , [FIG:ba3a01b0ff80af405db0] , and the same precoding being used within a REG bundle.

[FIG:a4953b03b61bdaeb4b82]

where the scrambling sequence [FIG:33f5f744d1d9d70fd3bd] is given by clause 5.2. The scrambling sequence shall be initialized with [FIG:daf11d319fde4eb6844a] at the start of each SS/PBCH block where

with [FIG:04e6d451b59b34122933] being the maximum number of SS/PBCH blocks in an SS/PBCH period for a particular band as given by [38.104].

[FIG:8ca50c7ac780258dec57]

where [FIG:6faf46a7bf73181e132c] , [FIG:5bb05f57b8c4507d98e1] , [FIG:9d5790ccebe9d059f2be] , and [FIG:9c43bc3d3f5d6097c23b] are given by Tables 7.4.1.1.2-1 and 7.4.1.1.2-2 and the following conditions are fulfilled:

- the resource elements are within the common resource blocks allocated for PDSCH transmission

The reference point for [FIG:92ec09558dafd5e66d65] is

- for PDSCH transmission carrying SIB1, subcarrier 0 of the lowest-numbered common resource block in the CORESET configured by the PBCH

- otherwise, subcarrier 0 in common resource block 0

The reference point for [FIG:d4e00ad22c3c9bb0b7a9] and the position [FIG:cfeb9c645a5cfd620fe7] of the first DM-RS symbol depends on the mapping type:

- for PDSCH mapping type A:

- [FIG:d5f18fe2e7cea5b82906] is defined relative to the start of the slot

- [FIG:f157002c5e98026ffa38] if the higher-layer parameter dmrs-TypeA-Position equals 3 and [FIG:d93555d8336a80f76bdb] otherwise

- [FIG:990ab6c8c0c65ebbdfa0] is defined relative to the start of the scheduled PDSCH resources

- [FIG:119a90cb830dc4b570cf]

- for PDSCH mapping type A, the duration is between the first OFDM symbol of the slot and the last OFDM symbol of the scheduled PDSCH resources in the slot

- for PDSCH mapping type B, the duration is the number of OFDM symbols of the scheduled PDSCH resources as signalled

and according to Tables 7.4.1.1.2-3 and 7.4.1.1.2-4. The case dmrs-AdditionalPositionequal to 3 is only supported when dmrs-TypeA-Position is equal to 2. For PDSCH mapping type B

- if the PDSCH duration is 2, 4, or 7 OFDM symbols for normal cyclic prefix or 2, 4, 6 OFDM symbols for extended cyclic prefix, and the PDSCH allocation collides with resources reserved for a CORESET, [FIG:19af5c40265b2c9dcf65] shall be incremented such that the first DM-RS symbol occurs immediately after the CORESETand

- if the PDSCH duration is 4 symbols, the UE is not expected to receive a DM-RS symbol beyond the third symbol,

- if the PDSCH duration is 7 symbols for normal cyclic prefix or 6 symbols for extended cyclic prefix,

- the UE is not expected to receive the first DM-RS beyond the fourth symbol, and

- if one additional single-symbol DM-RS is configured, the UE only expects the additional DM-RS to be transmitted on the 5th or 6th symbol when the front-loaded DM-RS symbol is in the 1st or 2nd symbol, respectively, of the PDSCH duration, otherwise the UE should expect that the additional DM-RS is not transmitted.

- if the PDSCH duration is 2 or 4 OFDM symbols, only single-symbol DM-RS is supported.

The time-domain index [FIG:b824e8357570a8979d4d] and the supported antenna ports [FIG:d1dc91f059fc43973323] are given by Table 7.4.1.1.2-5 where

- single-symbol DM-RS is used if the higher-layer parameter maxLength in the DMRS-DownlinkConfig IE is equal to 1

- single-symbol or double-symbol DM-RS is determined by the associated DCI if the higher-layer parameter maxLength in the DMRS-DownlinkConfig IE is equal to 2.

In absence of CSI-RS configuration, and unless otherwise configured, the UE may assume PDSCH DM-RS and SS/PBCH block to be quasi co-located with respect to Doppler shift, Doppler spread, average delay, delay spread, and, when applicable, spatial Rx parameters. The UE may assume that the PDSCH DM-RS within the same CDM group are quasi co-located with respect to Doppler shift, Doppler spread, average delay, delay spread, and spatial Rx. The UE may assume that DMRS ports associated with a PDSCH are QCL with QCL Type A, Type D (when applicable) and average gain.

The UE may assume that no DM-RS collides with the SS/PBCH block.

7.4.1.5 CSI reference signals

7.4.1.5.1 General

Zero-power (ZP) and non-zero-power (NZP) CSI-RS are defined

- for a non-zero-power CSI-RS configured by the NZP-CSI-RS-Resource IE, the sequence shall be generated according to clause 7.4.1.5.2 and mapped to resource elements according to clause 7.4.1.5.3

- for a zero-power CSI-RS configured by the ZP-CSI-RS-Resource IE, the UE shall assume that the resource elements defined in clause 7.4.1.5.3 are not used for PDSCH transmission. The UE performs the same measurement/reception on channels/signals except PDSCH regardless of whether they collide with ZP CSI-RS or not.

7.4.1.5.2 Sequence generation

The UE shall assume the reference-signal sequence [FIG:dddac2d874a8364eee39] is defined by

[FIG:f0035c9964f1d99991fc]

where the pseudo-random sequence [FIG:162dd46be5dd2a35896b] is defined in clause 5.2.1. The pseudo-random sequence generator shall be initialised with

[EQ:d288112343ed869aa1b4]

at the start of each OFDM symbol where [EQ:1327a493c04827e9a301] is the slot number within a radio frame, [FIG:bf0eb7a631646d37474b] is the OFDM symbol number within a slot, and [FIG:2a16c986060541b6b4fe] equals the higher-layer parameter scramblingID.

7.4.3 SS/PBCH block

7.4.3.1 Time-frequency structure of an SS/PBCH block

In the time domain, an SS/PBCH block consists of 4 OFDM symbols, numbered in increasing order from 0 to 3 within the SS/PBCH block, where PSS, SSS, and PBCH with associated DM-RS are mapped to symbols as given by Table 7.4.3.1-1.

In the frequency domain, an SS/PBCH block consists of 240 contiguous subcarriers with the subcarriers numbered in increasing order from 0 to 239 within the SS/PBCH block. The quantities [FIG:b9245ede54dbdc23955c] and [FIG:3439ac36e01f73f45513] represent the frequency and time indices, respectively, within one SS/PBCH block. The UE may assume that the complex-valued symbols corresponding to resource elements denoted as 'Set to 0' in Table 7.4.3.1-1 are set to zero. The quantity [FIG:e809c4751f7cdf5fab3e] in Table 7.4.3.1-1 is given by [FIG:4f947c80076d7f54ceab] . The quantity [FIG:c508027b5615a15af3d3] is the subcarrier offset from subcarrier 0 in common resource block [FIG:55a776315c072bd1326c] to subcarrier 0 of the SS/PBCH block, where the 4 least significant bits of [FIG:cdc4d4fc6742378ef402] are given by the higher-layer parameter ssb-SubcarrierOffset and for SS/PBCH block type A the most significant bit of [FIG:d8f40cdf43e398350d5b] is given by [FIG:be653eacf41dd232f496] in the PBCH payload as defined in subclause 7.1.1 of [4, TS 38.212]. If ssb-SubcarrierOffset is not provided, [EQ:132ab7566db5293a4f32] is derived from the frequency difference between the SS/PBCH block and Point A.

The UE may assume that the complex-valued symbols corresponding to resource elements that are part of a common resource block partially or fully overlapping with an SS/PBCH block and not used for SS/PBCH transmission are set set to zero in the OFDM symbols where SS/PBCH block is transmitted.

For an SS/PBCH block, the UE shall assume

- antenna port [FIG:5f20072bdc5e34583db4] is used for transmission of PSS, SSS and PBCH,

- the same cyclic prefix length and subcarrier spacing for the PSS, SSS, and PBCH,

- for SS/PBCH block type A, [FIG:ee0c4ba26d81876003a4] and [FIG:0dc3aa09bfc805a3c0e8] with the quantities [FIG:e30b915014f29798d035] , and [FIG:8ecfa7fd88fffc56dfc5] expressed in terms of 15 kHz subcarrier spacing, and

- for SS/PBCH block type B, [FIG:91c1ba2ca31d4120ef84] and [FIG:02a9c377535045b4656a] with the quantity [FIG:435f63e5ff70506b38fc] expressed in terms of the subcarrier spacing provided by the higher-layer parameter subCarrierSpacingCommon and [FIG:baf3e2739b5ed1ea7801] is expressed in terms of 60 kHz subcarrier spacing.

The UE may assume that SS/PBCH blocks transmitted with the same block index on the same center frequency location are quasi co-located with respect to Doppler spread, Doppler shift, average gain, average delay, delay spread, and, when applicable, spatial Rx parameters. The UE shall not assume quasi co-location for any other SS/PBCH block transmissions.

Table 7.4.3.1-1: Resources within an SS/PBCH block for PSS, SSS, PBCH, and DM-RS for PBCH.

Channel or signal | OFDM symbol number [FIG:0bbf0d1e51bf2c4508b7] 
relative to the start of an SS/PBCH block | Subcarrier number [FIG:d227770c6fce86982979] 
relative to the start of an SS/PBCH block
PSS | 0 | 56, 57, …, 182
SSS | 2 | 56, 57, …, 182
Set to 0 | 0 | 0, 1, …, 55, 183, 184, …, 239
 | 2 | 48, 49, …, 55, 183, 184, …, 191
PBCH | 1, 3 | 0, 1, …, 239
 | 2 | 0, 1, …, 47, 
192, 193, …, 239

7.4.3.1.1 Mapping of PSS within an SS/PBCH block

The UE shall assume the sequence of symbols [FIG:69c689a9e8b1baa79df4] constituting the primary synchronization signal to be scaled by a factor [FIG:2defae4e1883026883e7] to conform to the PSS power allocation specified in [5, TS 38.213] and mapped to resource elements [FIG:5eb2fbf65b8a5e38cc7d] in increasing order of [FIG:cf0d4e97f6930b144a1a] where [FIG:31dc67bd1b91efe70191] and [FIG:5408e2fe786b91a75581] are given by Table 7.4.3.1-1 and represent the frequency and time indices, respectively, within one SS/PBCH block.

7.4.3.1.2 Mapping of SSS within an SS/PBCH block

The UE shall assume the sequence of symbols [FIG:43aa721356624e2df029] constituting the secondary synchronization signal to be scaled by a factor [FIG:8fab42b5293080978bf2] and mapped to resource elements [FIG:93798c86b1b89e8b4c1e] in increasing order of [FIG:059d9058551ecf4de6f9] where [FIG:300daa19f051b843e0f8] and [FIG:9d3ed35b5e9354baeb6c] are given by Table 7.4.3.1-1 and represent the frequency and time indices, respectively, within one SS/PBCH block.

7.4.3.1.3 Mapping of PBCH and DM-RS within an SS/PBCH block

The UE shall assume the sequence of complex-valued symbols [FIG:a0e92b71f3f32a1d4b48] constituting the physical broadcast channel to be scaled by a factor [FIG:74c76d6d8d8b5453cfbc] to conform to the PBCH power allocation specified in [5, TS 38.213] and mapped in sequence starting with [FIG:1d5ce6d64d0a7114ba59] to resource elements [FIG:3581d6cb76728a419a28] which meet all the following criteria:

- they are not used for PBCH demodulation reference signals

The mapping to resource elements [FIG:1956c1e9c91e6f99f7d7] not reserved for PBCH DM-RS shall be in increasing order of first the index [FIG:c5153650e885c28132ad] and then the index [FIG:764c3fc7499a9b8046cb] , where [FIG:124c4d52ebf34fdc0e50] and [FIG:3ebb683b8acaaa79fb19] represent the frequency and time indices, respectively, within one SS/PBCH block and are given by Table 7.4.3.1-1.

The UE shall assume the sequence of complex-valued symbols [FIG:ce40ba4f6df674b99484] constituting the demodulation reference signals for the SS/PBCH block to be scaled by a factor of [FIG:5ba0ee5afbc6e90b7e57] to conform to the PBCH power allocation specified in [5, TS 38.213] and to be mapped to resource elements [FIG:670a691da9c6027b54ba] in increasing order of first [FIG:f2942a8e6d9b1cdc22c7] and then [FIG:176ccaab7316fc9d99c1] where [FIG:cf7f08358dbbf26d5435] and [FIG:3052035f350e62f26e65] are given by Table 7.4.3.1-1 and represent the frequency and time indices, respectively, within one SS/PBCH block.

7.4.3.2 Time location of an SS/PBCH block

The locations in the time domain where a UE shall monitor for a possible SS/PBCH block are described in clause 4.1 of [5, TS 38.213].
